# Open verification register

The single list of everything that still needs verification, with how to close it. Each chapter keeps its own "Not covered" section; this file collects them, so nothing depends on remembering which chapter said what.

**Rule (set by the project owner, 2026-11-11):** whenever a check is skipped, only partly done, done with an aid, or a result is unexplained, add a line here at once. When an item is closed, move it to "Closed" with the date and the evidence file. Review this file before claiming that any area is verified.

Status: **OPEN** (not verified), **PARTIAL** (some of it verified, the rest listed), **CLOSED** (moved to the end).
Method limits that apply to everything: all runs are on Altirra 4.40, NTSC, 800 with OS-B; real hardware and PAL were not tested (OV-01).

A replay has one `CONSOL` value per frame and cannot express it; the C takes one fixed answer, which is one of the ROM's; the generator keeps the inputs out of these races. ## 2. Sound (`21-start-gameover-title.md`, audit 2026-11-11)

Method: Altirra's real audio output recorded to WAV (`tools/record_tune.ps1`), register writes traced with exact emulated time (`tools/altirra_tune_record.cmd`), compared by `tools/analyze_tail_sound.py` and `tools/analyze_tune_wav.py`. The project owner listened to the start tune and confirmed it sounds correct; **no further listening is to be requested** (everything is to be confirmed by measurement).

| C-26 | **OV-42c: a fire on the cycle of a write that CHANGES `AUDCTL` gives no edge, exactly like one on the cycle of a changing `AUDF` write (OV-13c).** The bad sweep (PAL OS ROM, frame 5295) writes `AUDCTL` := `$84` 12 cycles before channel 4's tone entry, and channel 4 has a (poly5-gated) fire on that very cycle. The rule took that fire as the last sampling fire (poly9 bit 1); without it the last gated fire is 56 cycles earlier (bit 0), which gives the constant the recording needs (forcing it had fitted 0.998). Source (`pokey.cpp`, `case 0x08`): an `AUDCTL` write that changes the value runs `UpdateTimerCounter` and `FlushDeferredTimerEvents` for all four timers - the mechanism of the `AUDF` case. Test of the rule before adopting it (no fit): applied to every ghost-eaten sweep of all seven sessions (110 channel-sweeps NTSC incl. 14+14+9+1 sweeps, 66 PAL) it changes **exactly one** start bit, the bad one; and the same fire in sweep 3944 (an identical write/fire layout, but the fire is not poly5-gated) changes nothing, as it must. Implemented in `tools/pokey_timers.py` (`Timer(..., ctl_changes)`: those fires are dropped from `edges`; the timer keeps its schedule) and `tools/analyze_ghost_sweep.py` (`Model` passes the cycles of the writes that change AUDCTL). Second item: the 15th window of the PAL-OS recording (0.000 s, ratio 4.6) was a start-up spike of the band-pass / AC filters at sample 0 of the arrays (0.01-0.02, gone in 5 ms) where the recording is inaudible; `analyze_full_waveform.py` now skips window 0 when the recording is inaudible there. **Result: PAL OS ROM 4,345 of 4,345 windows >= 0.90 (min 0.9934, median 0.9989; absolute scale 0.995)**; NTSC sound-events 3,117 (min 0.9875), events2 3,167, level 12 2,731, natural 845, PAL 4,247, HLE 4,067: unchanged, all 100 %. Tests: `test_audctl_change_drops_the_coincident_fire` (`tests/test_pokey_timers.py`), and `test_ghost_sweep_model`, `test_hp`, `test_pokey_noise`, `test_full_waveform`, `test_sound_analyzers` (14) pass. Limit: only one real case of the rule exists in the data (it is the only fire that coincides with an `AUDCTL` change AND is gated and last); the source mechanism and the zero side effects on 175 other channel-sweeps are the support. | CLOSED | |
| C-27 | **OV-42b: closed as a documented limit, by the owner's decision (2026-11-13). It is NOT a condition for the 100 % decompilation of the ROM.** What is NOT verified on real hardware: the sound's pitch, level and timing are checked against Altirra 4.40's audio path only (NTSC and PAL timing, OS-B NTSC / OS-B PAL ROM, Altirra's built-in OS). What the limit does and does not touch: the ROM's own sound code and its register writes (every one of the 31 write sites, the values, the timing in frames) are verified from the ROM and traces alone and do not depend on the emulator's audio path; only the claim 'it sounds exactly like this on a real machine' rests on the emulator. Should a real-hardware capture ever exist, the start-tune audit (`tools/analyze_tune_wav.py` on the WAV alone: pitch of the 64 steps, step length, voice levels) would be the first check; no further work is planned. | CLOSED (limit) | |
| C-28 | **OV-43: the tones the spectral audit cannot judge by pitch are judged in the waveform domain - the recording prefers the true model to a model with the tone 20 cents off in 341 of 341 tested unresolved tones.** Why the spectral audit leaves them: in a 12-13 ms window a weak voice under a louder one, or the onset of another channel, hides the pitch (it is 'not resolved', level still checked). New `tools/check_segments_waveform.py` (summaries in `analysis/evidence-ov43-*-segments.txt`) takes the audit's segment list and the waveform model (`analyze_full_waveform.py`): (1) the correlation of the recording with the model over EVERY segment (silence skipped), limit 0.90: **100 % in all six sessions** (NTSC sound-events 8,163 segments, min 0.977; level 12 7,472, min 0.936; natural 1,935, min 0.998; HLE 10,711, min 0.971; PAL 10,253, min 0.997; PAL OS ROM 10,520, min 0.992), including all 40 / 53 / 122 / 61 / 56 / 58 unresolved ones and all flagged ones; (2) the drift (lag in the first against the second half of the segment), max 0.0-8.3 cents, descriptive only: it follows the loudest voice and so cannot judge a weak one (unit-tested: a weak voice 20 cents off does not move it); (3) **the shift test**, which does judge a weak voice: the model is re-rendered with ONE channel's edges in the segment stretched by +-c cents, and the recording must correlate better with the true model. Result for the unresolved tones outside the ghost-eaten sweeps: **at 20 cents 36/36, 47/47, 122/122, 54/54, 38/38, 44/44 = 341/341**; at 10 cents 337/341; at 5 cents 305/341 (natural session 122/122 even at 5 cents); at 40 cents 341/341 with an advantage of at least +0.006. So the resolution is about 10 cents - better than the spectral limits (30 cents long tones, 80 cents short) - and no tone prefers a shifted model. The 13 channel items of flagged segments outside sweeps pass the same way (13/13 at 20 cents). The 4 items on the natural session: correlation 1.000, drift max 0.2 cents, and the one channel item is shift-tested 1/1 even at 5 cents: they were artifacts of the 12 ms spectral window, as judged before, now measured. Tests: `LagDrift` (3), `SegmentsOnRealSession` (the real session passes with the shift test at 36/36, and a trace whose siren `AUDF2` values are damaged by +1 in every third write fails with exit 1) in `tests/test_sound_analyzers.py` (now 19 tests). **Limit (closed in C-29):** 49 unresolved tones and 70 flagged channel items lie INSIDE ghost-eaten sweeps (coherent equal-pitch voices of channels 1/3/4); the shift test is not run there (the sweep model is a different renderer); their correlation (>= 0.97) and drift are in the evidence, their pitch rests on the sweep verification of OV-13/OV-14 (edge timing 99-100 % within 0.5 ms, fast-sweep pitch) and the exact timers (16,364 of 16,364 dump readings). | CLOSED | |
| C-29 | **OV-43, the sweep limit of C-28 closed: the shift test now also runs inside the ghost-eaten sweeps.** `tools/check_segments_waveform.py` renders a segment that lies inside a sweep with the sweep model itself (`Model.mixed` with the high-pass latch and the constants `c1`, `c3`, `c4` of that sweep, exposed by `analyze_full_waveform.py` as `sweep_params`), once as it is and once with the fires of the one channel inside the segment stretched by +-c cents, and the recording must correlate better with the true one. Result over the six sessions (390 unresolved tones, 83 channel items of flagged segments, nothing left untested): **at 20 cents 390/390 and 83/83**; at 10 cents 386/390 (unresolved) and 82/83; at 40 cents all. Inside the sweeps the margin is large, because a stretched voice of a coherent equal-pitch group breaks the high-pass pair: the 20-cent advantage is +0.07 to +0.36 (median ~ +0.2), against +0.002 to +0.02 outside; at 5 cents all sweep tones pass except one (HLE, -0.008). Sweep tones: 4+6+0+7+18+14 = 49 unresolved and 0+0+0+0+35+35 = 70 flagged items. Evidence: `analysis/evidence-ov43-*-segments.txt` (regenerated). Unit tests unchanged and passing (19). OV-43 has no exception left. | CLOSED | |
| C-30 | **OV-44: the 0.5 gain is Altirra's `Audio: Volume` setting, and the last 0.5 % was the continuous-time AC coupling of the model plus the droop of the model's box filter; the absolute level now matches to 0.1 %.** (1) **The gain, located.** `settings.cpp` reads `Audio: Volume` with the default `0.5f`; `Altirra_record.ini` holds `"Audio: Volume" = 1056964608` = `0x3F000000` = 0.5f; `ATAudioOutput::SetVolume` passes it to `ATAudioFilter::mScale`, which multiplies the signal in the high-pass stage (`audiofilters.cpp`, `*dst++ = v1 * scale`). Nothing else in the path scales it: the resampler's kernel is normalised (row sums 0.99994..1.00012 of 16384; 32767/16384 turns it into 16-bit samples), the 15 kHz FIR and the 24 kHz band-limited step table each have unit DC gain. So `--absolute-scale` = volume 0.5 x slope 2.1 x UNIT / 2 is exact, not a measured constant (it follows the user's volume setting). (2) **The 0.5 %, explained in two parts.** The model/recording transfer function, measured over the whole sound-events session (cross-spectrum, 100-5000 Hz, power-weighted): 0.9970 at 150-300 Hz falling to 0.9746 at 4-5 kHz, 0.9947 overall. (a) The AC coupling: Altirra applies TWO discrete one-pole high-pass stages at its 63,920 Hz sample rate - the POKEY reference decay (`pokey.cpp` / `PostFilter`: coefficient `exp(-1/(63920 tau))`, tau = 2.5968 ms) and the audio filter's own (`mHiCoeff` = 0.000634131) - H(z) = (1 - z^-1)/(1 - c z^-1); the model used the continuous-time `jw tau/(1 + jw tau)`, which is 0.36 % lower at 200 Hz (0.04 % at 1 kHz). `band(..., ac=True)` in `analyze_full_waveform.py` now uses the two discrete stages (`--continuous-ac` restores the old form); the flat part of the deficit vanished (150-300 Hz: 1.0000). (b) The droop of the model's own 44.1 kHz box average against Altirra's chain (24 kHz band-limited step table x 15 kHz FIR x 63.9->44.1 kHz interpolation kernel, all taken from the source and evaluated numerically): box/chain = 0.9993 at 1 kHz, 0.997 at 2 kHz, 0.993 at 3 kHz, 0.983 at 5 kHz. Power-weighted by the session's own spectrum the chain predicts 0.9987; measured after (a): **0.9979**. Left: 0.08 % (mostly at 4-5 kHz, 0.4 % of the model's power; not chased). **Result: the RMS ratio model / recording with `--absolute-scale` is 0.999 (events, events2, level 12, natural, HLE, PAL) and 0.998 (PAL OS ROM)** instead of 0.995; the per-volume start-tune calibration is 0.963 (it reflects the mixer's uneven volume-bit weights, OV-15). Every window count is unchanged: 3,117 / 3,167 / 2,731 / 845 / 4,067 / 4,247 / 4,345 windows, all >= 0.90 (evidence `analysis/evidence-ov44-*-absscale.txt`). The correlation medians are unchanged to the 4th digit. (3) **'Short-segment limits (OV-14 a-c)'**: those limits are the spectral audit's (12-13 ms windows); OV-14d/e (C-13..C-15) replaced them by the waveform comparison, and OV-43 (C-28, C-29) judged every segment of the audit's list and every unresolved or flagged tone in the waveform domain. Nothing of OV-14 a-c is left. Tests: `DiscreteAc` (the filter equals the difference equation and is not the continuous form) and `AbsoluteScaleOnRealSession` (ratio 1.000 +- 0.003) in `tests/test_sound_analyzers.py` (now 21 tests). | CLOSED | |

| C-40 | **OV-48: the ratio is -0.1270 ppm, which is exactly 1,789,772.5 Hz against the model's 1,789,772.727 Hz, and it is in every long recording.** (1,789,772.5 - 1,789,772.7273) / 1,789,772.7273 = -0.1270 ppm; 1,789,772.5 Hz is 3,579,545 Hz (the rounded NTSC colour-burst clock) / 2. `tools/ov48_ratio_table.py` ran every NTSC recording of 170 s or more (`evidence-ov48-ratio-table.txt`) at the old default and with `--rate-ppm -0.1270`, and measured the ratio with `probe_wave_drift.py`: **sound-events -0.1268 ppm, sound-events2 -0.1273, C-36 -0.1267, C-37 -0.1274, the built-in-OS session -0.1211, sound-hp -0.1170** (all with a fit residual of 0.01-0.08 sample); **after applying -0.1270 the fitted ratio is 0.0004, 0.0004, 0.0000, -0.0000, -0.0002 ppm** (events, events2, C-37, sound-hp, C-35) and every window passes: C-33 2,913 of 2,913 (median 0.9995, min 0.9962; was 0.9911 / 0.9513), C-34 4,218 of 4,218 (min 0.9953; was 4,156, min 0.8908), C-35 4,415 of 4,415 (min 0.9958; was 4,405), C-36 9,515 of 9,515 (min 0.9931; was 5,583), C-37 8,978 of 8,978 (min 0.9955; was 6,399), sound-events2 min 0.9983 (was 0.9332), sound-hp 4,048 of 4,048 (min 0.9956; was 4,046). So the `--anchor-samples 1` of C-34 / C-35 and the late windows of C-34 were this ratio. **`analyze_full_waveform.py` now applies -0.1270 ppm by default for NTSC** (`AUDIO_RATIO_PPM_NTSC`, pinned in `tests/test_sound_bot.py`; `--rate-ppm 0` gives the old model, `--rate-ppm x` any other); PAL keeps 0 (not measured). Everything quoted in C-13 to C-35 was produced with the old default and stays as written; each figure is the same or better with the new one. One recording got a lower median with the ratio: sound-events (C-13, 176 s; 0.9991 / min 0.9881 at the old default, 0.9951 / 0.9793 with the ratio). **Cause found: the anchor, not the ratio.** At the old default its lag line ran from -0.57 to +0.21 samples (it crossed zero in the middle of the recording, which hid an anchor that is 0.7 sample off: the anchor is found to ONE sample); the ratio removes the slope and leaves the 0.7. `--anchor-samples` now takes a fraction: **with the ratio and `--anchor-samples -0.7`, sound-events gives median 0.9999, min 0.9995 in all 3,117 windows (the best of all its runs)**, and `+0.7` gives 0.9783 / 0.9096, as expected. `probe_wave_drift.py` now also prints the anchor correction it implies. The model's anchor is now also kept at the anchor sample when the ratio changes (`c0 = c_w2 - t_w2 * rate * step`), which is the same as before at ratio 0. **Limits:** Altirra's source was not read (the web search found nothing; the source is not on a public repository), so '1,789,772.5 Hz in the audio path' is an inference from a number that matches to the fourth digit in four independent recordings, and a sample rate of 44,100.0056 Hz would give the same ratio; the tool needs only the ratio. The three PAL recordings (`ov42-*-events`) cannot be analysed by the default settings of the tool (the PAL ones need the options of C-24 / C-25), so a PAL ratio was not measured. For the 1,500 s recordings and for C-33 and C-36 the probe's per-block lag is ambiguous in long steady tones (residual of 4-7 samples), so their ratio is not taken from the probe; their windows pass at 0.99 all the same. | 2026-11-13 | `evidence-ov48-ratio-table.txt`, `tools/ov48_ratio_table.py` |

| C-44 | **OV-22: nothing can happen later at the title in state 4, by the code: no routine that runs in state 4 reads a timer or a counter, so a longer wait cannot differ from the 90,632 frames already observed.** All code that runs while `zp_19` = 4 (`src/asm/pacman.s`): (1) the **main loop** `$A4BA-$A504`: `zp_19` <> 0 jumps at `$A4CA` over the `CH`/`zp_9B` code to `$A4F5`, which writes `CH` := `$FF` and reads only `STRIG0`, `STRIG1` (a press starts a game at `$A537`) and `CONSOL` (`$A504`; `$07` loops back to `$A4BA`); `zp_20` / `zp_1E` are not read in state 4 (the countdown test `$A4E9-$A4F3` is bypassed), so the countdown cannot restart; (2) the **deferred VBI** `Sub_A6CC`: state 4 takes `$A719-$A78B`, which only redraws the title text from constants and reads `zp_1A` and `StartLevel`; the one use of `RTCLOK2` there (`$A713`) belongs to state 2 and the one in `$A78C-$A796` (the countdown) to state 0 (`zp_19` = 0), both unreachable in state 4; (3) the **DLIs** (`$A573`, `$A5CE`) only set colours and `CHBASE` from `DliColors` and `zp_18`. The only state that changes with time is the OS's `ATRACT` (an OS function, not game code; the game reads `COLRSH`/`DRKMSK` that the OS derives from it for the colours): +1 about every 256 frames, 254 at the end of the 90,632-frame wait (it did not wrap, or the colours would have returned). The wait already exceeded 65,536 frames (a 16-bit counter wrap) and `RTCLOK` (24 bits, 3.3 days) is not read in state 4. Live evidence unchanged: four waits (200, 90,632, 200 and 36,994 frames), only `ATRACT` and the debounce counter changed (`21`). Limit: no longer session was run; the proof is by code reading (`$A4C8-$A559`, `$A6CC-$A78B`, the DLIs) and the OS behaviour of `ATRACT` is observed, not read from the OS ROM. | 2026-11-13 | `docs/21-start-gameover-title.md`, `src/asm/pacman.s` |

| C-54 | **Stage 44 and OV-54: the program uses the hardware stack only, every routine is balanced, and the stack never needs more than 28 bytes of the 256 (21 measured in the deepest case, which the model predicts exactly).** `tools/analyze_stack.py` (`docs/28-stack.md`, `tests/test_stack.py`, 14 tests): no instruction addresses page 1, no `TSX`, no indirect jump; 8 `PHA` / `PHP` pushes (each with its pop), `TXS` once (S = `$FF`); all 81 routines leave the stack as they found it. Measurement: `tools/run_stack_audit.ps1` runs five session harnesses (about 590,000 VBIs, 3.0 million display-list interrupts; the death harness refills lives, an old test aid) with a trace of S, the scan line and the **interrupted pc read from the stack** at every interrupt entry (`tools/analyze_stack_log.py` -> `analysis/evidence-stack-summary.txt`). Results: (1) the layout is as assumed: the interrupted pc is a real instruction of the cartridge at 593,362 of 593,362 VBI entries (so the OS pushes exactly 3 bytes before the VBI handler) and the display-list interrupts see 3 bytes (hardware only); no interrupt ever arrived inside a handler (0 of 3,560,181). (2) The deepest chain `FrameDispatch -> TurnControl -> InitBoard -> ClearPlayerMemory -> FillPlayerPages` ran 21 times inside the VBI (16 bytes in use, the main program idle); `DliHandler1` hit inside it 50 times (19 bytes at entry, 21 with its own pushes: lowest write `$01EB`), which equals the model 6 + 10 + 3 + 2. The first version of OV-54 wrongly said this case was not reached: it had added the largest VBI entry (10, from a different situation) to the deepest chain. (3) What was not observed: `DliHandler2` inside that chain (+1 byte; it cannot occur: `SetDisplayList` writes the display list and `VDSLST` together, the game screen uses handler 1 (`$A49B`), the title screen handler 2 (`$A621`); in 1,182,300 entries handler 2 was never deeper than 5 bytes), and the main program 4-6 bytes deep when the deep chain runs (measured, the main program was 2 or 4 bytes deep only inside its own `Copy` routines (pc `$B124-$B176`) and idle in its key loop otherwise; at all 25 deep-chain events it was idle). Both are bounded by construction: Start 6 + VBI entry 6 + VbiHandler 10 + 3 + 3 = 28 bytes. No stack overflow is possible, and nothing needs to be measured further. | 2026-11-14 | `analysis/evidence-stack-summary.txt`; raw logs `analysis/raw-logs/stack-*.txt` (local; the first run in `stack-run1/`). |

Prototype: unevenness 2.7 (game) -> 1.3 (before) -> 0.34 (1 frame ahead) / 0.26 (2 ahead); the prediction differs from the exact way by 0.1 pixels on average, 0.56 at the 95th percentile, 2 pixels at the 99th. | C-115 | **Stage 80c (docs/51): the audio. The POKEY model in C, from timed register writes to PCM, reproduces Altirra's recordings; the port's front end gives the core's writes their cycles. NTSC.** `src/c/audio/pokey.[ch]`: a forward, event-driven model of the chip as the game uses it, every rule taken from docs/22 and its tools (`pokey_timers.py`, `pokey_noise.py`, `analyze_ghost_sweep.py`, `analyze_full_waveform.py`): the four timers on the 28-cycle grid (reload with the AUDF at the fire; a write on the cycle of a fire comes after it); a fire is processed in the AUDC / AUDCTL 2 cycles after it (pure tone toggles the flip-flop, noise sets it to the bit of the 17-bit / 9-bit counter, gated by the 5-bit one; counters free-running from the SKCTL write); a fire on the cycle of a write that changes AUDF, or a write that changes AUDCTL, gives no edge; the high-pass pair (AUDCTL bit 2: channel 1 XOR the latch that channel 3 clocks); Altirra's non-linear mixer indexed by the channels that are LOW; the AC coupling (two discrete high-pass stages at 63,920 Hz); the 28-cycle samples box-averaged into the output rate. The polarity of a tone is the RULE (the flip-flop's own state), nothing is fitted. `audio.[ch]`: `audio_frame(World)` takes the log of the sound-chip writes of a frame, empties it, stamps the writes (`write_clock.[ch]`: first write 40 cycles after the frame start, 9 cycles apart: the measured medians, `tools/measure_write_clock.py`), runs the chip and returns the frame's PCM (about 736 samples at 44,100 Hz; the audio lags the frame by 1,000 cycles). **Checked, exact cycles** (`tools/check_audio_c.py`, `tests/test_audio.py`: the writes of the traces with their recorded cycles go through the C program `tests/audio_check`, the signal is band-passed 100 Hz - 5 kHz and compared with Altirra's recording in 50 ms windows at lag 0, as docs/22 does): **`sound-events` 3,117 of 3,117 windows >= 0.90 (median 0.9951, min 0.979), `sound-events2` 3,167 of 3,167 (median 0.9993), `sound-level12` 2,731 of 2,731 (min 0.991), `sound-hp` (the ghost-eaten sweeps) 4,048 of 4,048 (min 0.992), `sound-natural` 845 of 845 (median 1.0000)**; RMS ratio C / recording 0.998 in every recording (the level comes from Altirra's own constants, `--absolute-scale`); against the Python model of docs/22 the median correlation is 1.0000 (1 window of 4,048 differs in `sound-hp`: the Python model FITS the polarity of a run to the recording and the C rule is right there). **Checked, the port's front end** (the writes of a frame get their cycles from the write clock; the core knows none): the waveform has the pitch, level and timing of each frame of the original but not its phase, so the windows are compared by their spectra (magnitudes, 100 Hz - 5 kHz, phase ignored): **median 0.9997-0.9998, 97.6-98.5 % of the 50 ms windows have a spectrum correlation >= 0.90 with the recording** (`sound-events`, `-events2`, `-level12`, `-hp`), RMS ratio 0.998; the others are the ghost-eaten sweeps (channels 1, 3 and 4 at nearly the same pitch: their loudness depends on the phase between the channels, i.e. on the exact cycle of three writes, which a core without cycles cannot know) and a few windows at a tone change. A table of the MEASURED cycles of each of the 18 register sequences (the game's code paths) was tried: no better than the two-number rule (97.1-98.1 % held out against 97.6-98.5 %), so it was dropped. **Negative controls**: the noise counters started 7 cycles later (the polarity of tone runs changes), one channel's AUDF one step off, and every write 11 ms late each drop the windows far below 100 %. `ctest audio_selftest` (no files): the pitch of a tone (277.9 Hz from AUDF 114: zero crossings), its swing through the mixer table and the AC coupling (2 / (1 + exp(-T / 2 tau)) times the step, within 3 %), silence, determinism, a write on the cycle of a fire, the counters of what is not modelled, the front end (736 samples a frame, 441,543 for 600 frames, the log taken and emptied, non-sound addresses ignored). Clang and gcc, 35 of 35 `ctest`. **Limits**: not modelled because the game does not use it (counted in `unsupported`): AUDCTL bits 0, 3, 4, 5, 6, the poly4 modes, volume-only mode; PAL (owner: NTSC only); the output is a box filter of the 28-cycle samples (aliasing above the band is not removed; the comparison is band-limited to 100 Hz - 5 kHz like docs/22's); the phase of the front end's output is not Altirra's (see above); the audio device, the sample rate chosen by the shell and the mixing with anything else are 80d (decision D3: the audio / window library is not chosen: `audio_frame` returns a plain float buffer). The recordings and traces are local evidence. Nothing committed. |
| C-114 | **Stage 80b (docs/51): the renderer. The picture of the screen from the World, in C, checked pixel by pixel against the real pictures of Altirra; NTSC only (owner, 2026-11-22).** `src/c/render/render.[ch]`: ANTIC (the display list in the DLIST shadow read from the ROM window, modes 2, 4, 6, 7 and blank lines, LMS, JMP / JVB, the interrupt bit; characters from the character set CHBASE selects: title set, game set, OS font page of the blob; mode 2 inverse / blank bits of CHACT) and GTIA (four players and four missiles from the player-missile pages and `HPOS`, `PRIOR` bits 0-3 and the fifth-player bit, the colour registers) to a 384 x 240 picture of GTIA colour bytes (scan lines 8-247, colour clocks 32-223); `render_to_rgb` through `palette.[ch]`. The display-list interrupts are what `DliHandler1` / `DliHandler2` write (read from `src/asm/pacman.s`, `docs/35`): CHBASE and COLPF0-3 / COLPM0-3 per band, effective from the line after the interrupt line, `BandCounter` from 0. The colours at the start of a frame are the OS shadows of the PREVIOUS frame (the OS copies them in the vertical blank before the game's handler changes them): `Renderer` keeps them from call to call (`render_reset`; tested). **The palette is measured, not computed (decision D2: NTSC as measured, no PAL):** `tools/measure_palette.py` builds three 6502 programs that show every colour byte as a stripe (no OS, no display list), `tools/run_palette_shots.ps1` captures Altirra's own window, the stripes are read in order from a known first colour: **128 of 128 colours, all different, 13 colours the game's own screens showed (C-50, C-52, the ghost captures) are where the measurement puts them**; `analysis/palette-ntsc-measured.txt`, `tools/make_c_palette.py` -> `render/palette.[ch]`. **Real pictures:** `tools/run_item_shots.ps1 -Cmd altirra_rendershots.cmd -FreezeAt a5fd` makes 12 captures (the bonus items of levels 0 / 6 / 12, the lives icons, two pop-ups, the one- and two-player titles, two two-player games, the four ghosts overlapping in both orders) with the whole RAM `$0000-$CFFF`, every write to `HPOSP / HPOSM / COLPM / PRIOR` and to the colour registers and CHBASE with its scan line; the machine is frozen at the entry of a frame handler and left running 90 video frames (a stub clears BandCounter, the stack is reset) so that the window shows the frame of the logged RAM. `tools/check_render_shots.py` builds the World from the log, draws the frame with the C program `tests/render_check` (tables, character sets and font installed from the ROM, C-112 / C-113), turns the colour bytes into RGB with the measured palette and compares every pixel of the 384 x 240 picture with the capture at the window pixel of the calibration of the ghost screenshots (x = 5.4680 * clock + 12.05, y = 3.1711 * line - 46.68; best whole-pixel shift: 0 in every capture): **12 captures, 1,073,664 pixels: 90.3 % exactly the expected RGB, 0.5 % the colour of a neighbouring pixel, 9.2 % scaling blends of the window, and 0 pixels of a wrong colour outside the players and missiles** (maze, status rows, text, title screens with the OS font, items, lives, the DLI bands, PRIOR order all exact); the two title captures 95.2 / 95.1 % exact. **At the sprites: 212 pixels of 1,073,664 differ in 5 of the 12 captures, 7 captures are exact everywhere** (`analysis/evidence-render-shots-summary.txt`). Those pixels are the edges and pictures of ghosts whose position or picture changed between the frame in the window and the logged frame (the window does not present every emulated frame at 800 % speed; the same capture set made twice gave different captures with the differences in different places, and a longer freeze did not remove them): they lie inside the region of a moving object (+-16 pixels, +-2 lines) and are reported apart. **Negative controls** (`tests/test_render.py`, 13 tests, clang and gcc): sprites four colour clocks off, another PRIOR, one changed screen cell, one changed colour shadow, a swapped palette entry each give wrong pixels; synthetic frames show the geometry (a player 8 colour clocks = 16 pixels wide starting at colour clock `HPOS`, a missile 2 clocks with its high bit on the left, PRIOR 1 / 2 / 4 / 8 put a player over or under a wall as the table says, the fifth player takes COLPF3, mode 7 rows are two lines per picture line, the DLI bands change the picture, the colours of a frame come from the shadows of the frame before). **Limits (written in `render.h`)**: not modelled because the game never uses it: narrow and wide playfield, double-line player-missile resolution, `SIZEP` / `SIZEM`, the multicolour and overlap bits of PRIOR, fine scrolling, vertical reflection, ANTIC modes other than 2, 4, 6, 7; the colours are Altirra 4.40's NTSC palette, not a real monitor's (the limit of C-27); the check against the window is a colour check at the calibrated pixel centres (scaling blends of the window make 9 % of the pixels unreadable as a colour); the sprites are exact in 7 of 12 captures and within one frame of motion in the rest; the audio and the shell are 80c and 80d; the CRT look of decision D2 is not made (the owner may ask for it). The capture programs and the pictures are local (`analysis/shots/`, git-ignored); the palette and the summary are committed evidence. Nothing committed. |

| C-16 | **OV-14d and OV-14e: the start state of a tone outside the sweep is the rule of OV-13a; the "chance" was a bug of the renderer, not of the rule.** `run_events` (`tools/analyze_full_waveform.py`) started a run at `noise bit ^ parity(all fires since reset) ^ 1`; that parity belongs to the *constant* of the sweep model (output = constant XOR parity of all fires), not to a state, so the polarity of every run was a coin toss (31 of 58 + 9 of 17). Found with a controlled test, `tools/start_state_test.py` (injected 6502 program, interrupts off, ROM untouched; per trial: channel idle `AUDC` $00 with `AUDF` Ai, 3 frames, tone `AUDC` $A4 started at a cycle that moves by 5 cycles per trial, a volume-only DC marker before each trial anchors the recording to the trace): 224 trials per channel on channels 1, 2, 3, 4 (idle `AUDF` 0 / 2 / 13 / 20, tone `AUDF` 13) plus 224 on channel 2 with tone `AUDF` 114 / 60 / 28 / 13. With the old line the rule was at chance everywhere (e.g. 123 of 224), but "wrong" was exactly the parity of the timer's fire count before the tone in **all** 224 trials of every run (0 of 224 or 224 of 224, never mixed). Fix: state = noise bit XOR 1 for channels 3/4 (the polarity fitted on the sweeps) and XOR 0 for channels 1/2 (their output is the flip-flop XOR the high-pass flip-flop, which sits at 1; read in `pokeyrenderer.cpp`, `mChannelOutputMask` bits 4-5). Limits: the output gain 0.5 was found from the measurement, not located in the source (the audio path scales the signal after `pokey.cpp`); the 0.5 % left over is unexplained. | C-25 | **OV-42a: the 2 odd windows of the built-in-OS session were a flaw of the analysis model, not of the game or the OS.** Method: the debugger's `.pokey` dump around the last `AUDF2` write and the silencing of channel 2 (`tools/altirra_ov42a.cmd`, `run_patrol_trace.ps1 -Kernel hle`, 133,381 SNDW lines identical to the recorded session, so the run is reproducible; the dump's `Output` turned out to be the renderer's state at its last flush, not the live flip-flop, so it cannot judge parity) did not help; the audio did: in both windows the model and the recording agree up to the ghost-eaten start (`$A8EE`, channel 2 silenced) and then the model is **inverted for the first half frame (~17 ms, until channel 4 enters at +29,000 cycles)**, correct afterwards. Both sweeps begin while channel 1 is ALREADY in a pure tone (the same state at f=3791 is also in the session and was right by luck). The sweep model took the flip-flop constant of channel 1 as 0 (right when channel 1 enters its tone inside the sweep: 87 of 87 earlier sweeps) - but a channel-1 tone that began earlier has the state of its own run (the start-state rule of OV-14d, fitted 24 of 24). **Fix** in `tools/analyze_full_waveform.py`: if channel 1 is in pure tone at the sweep start, its constant is derived from the state of that run (`c1 = state ^ parity of its fires`). Result: **HLE session 4,067 of 4,067 windows >= 0.90 (min 0.9817, median 0.9986)**; NTSC sound-events 3,117 of 3,117 (min 0.9875, unchanged), sound-events2 3,167/3,167, natural 845/845, PAL 4,247/4,247, level 12 2,731 of 2,731 (the old evidence file: 2,659 = 97.3 %, min -0.94, now min 0.9428); PAL OS ROM unchanged (OV-42c). Unit test `WaveformOnHleSession` (`tests/test_sound_analyzers.py`, 14 tests) pins it. Caveat for OV-42c: its bad sweep does NOT begin inside a channel-1 tone (channel-4 start bit) - a different cause. | CLOSED | |
